<summary>Agent "Review docstring_accuracy" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>The review found one false claim, so I did not call `mark_review_as_passed`.

**Finding: `/Users/hosanglee/archy/src/archy/install/base.py`, the new comment in `remove_instructions` (about lines 184-186)**

- **Claim:** "upsert_instructions put one blank line between the user's text and the block; take it back out so the strip is the exact inverse (#560)."
- **What the code does:** `upsert_instructions` adds a newline of its own when the existing text lacks a trailing one (`sep = "" if existing.endswith("\n") else "\n"`, then `f"{existing}{sep}\n{block}"`). `remove_instructions` strips only one of the resulting two newlines. For user text with no trailing newline, the round trip is therefore not an exact inverse. I ran both functions to confirm:
  - `'a'` becomes `'a\n'`.
  - `'a\n'`, `'a\n\n'` and `'x\r\ny\r\n'` all round-trip unchanged.
- **Why it matters here:** the `retire` path calls this function on existing global files, and it only skips the rewrite when the result equals the existing text. So a global `CLAUDE.md` whose user text has no trailing newline gets a trailing `\n` added when the old block is cleared. This also makes the `FileAction.retire` docstring claim that "a user's own text stays byte-identical" false for that case. That claim holds only for files that contain no archy text at all.
- **Rewrite for the comment:** "upsert_instructions put one blank line between the user's text and the block; take it back out so a file that ended in a newline round-trips exactly. A file with no trailing newline gains one, since upsert added it too."

I checked the other changed prose and found it true:
- `instructions_action` docstring
- `render_plan` and `write_rendered` docstrings (`WriteSystem.remove` exists)
- `preview_config` comment
- the `claude.py` global-plugin comment

One imprecision I did not count as a finding: "Uninstall always gets the plain strip" is loose for the Continue and Cursor adapters, where the unrender is `delete_file` rather than a strip.</result>

**Finding 1: DRY, copy-pasted block (lines 1763-1771 and 1821-1829)**
- **Criterion:** "DRY: no copy-pasted blocks that should be a shared helper".
- **Offending construct:** `compute_conventions` and `compute_module_view` each carry the same loop. It calls `_try_collect_detailed(module, root=root)`, then runs `assert miss is not None`, appends the miss to `skipped`, and collects the facts.
  - `compute_conventions` appends to a `list[_ModuleFacts]`.
  - `compute_module_view` stores into a `dict[str, _ModuleFacts]` keyed by qualname.
  - The `_try_collect_detailed` docstring says it exists so "the two callers cannot drift". The loop around it is still duplicated, so the two callers can drift on this part.
- **Suggested fix:** Extract one helper, for example `_collect_all(modules: Iterable[Module], root: Path) -&gt; tuple[list[_ModuleFacts], list[UnparsedModule]]`. It would hold the try-collect call, the assert and the skipped bookkeeping. `compute_module_view` can then build its dict with `{f.qualname: f for f in facts}`.

This is the only finding. File: `/Users/hosanglee/archy/src/archy/conventions.py`.</result>

The conventions file limits findings to what the changeset introduced or moved. `_Resolver` and `_FileIndex` come from the recent split refactor of `assemble_graph` and `suggest_files`.

1. **Line 392, `class _Resolver(NamedTuple)`**
   - Criterion: "Value objects use `pydantic.BaseModel` with `model_config = ConfigDict(frozen=True)` and tuple (not list) sequence fields."
   - Offending construct: a `NamedTuple` holding `qualname_set: set[str]` and `reexport_maps: dict[str, dict[str, str]]`.
   - The conventions file's only `NamedTuple` exception is `coupling.CoChangeData`. A new candidate must be huge, hot and never serialized, all three. This one is a single instance per graph build, so it is not hot.
   - Fix: make it a frozen `BaseModel` with `ConfigDict(frozen=True)`, with `frozenset[str]` for `qualname_set`. Pydantic validation of the nested dicts is cheap here because it is built once per build. Alternatively, pass the two values as plain arguments again.
   - Wrinkle: `set` and `dict` fields still leave the model mutable in place, so use `frozenset`. `_build_reexport_maps` also mutates its dicts, so keep `dict` for `reexport_maps` and accept that nested dicts stay mutable.

2. **Line 591, `class _FileIndex(NamedTuple)`**
   - Criterion: the same bullet.
   - Offending construct: a `NamedTuple` with `root: Path`, `by_qualname: dict[str, PurePosixPath]` and `longest_first: list[tuple[str, ...]]`. It is built once per `suggest_files` call, so it is not hot, and the `list` field is not a tuple.
   - Fix: make it a frozen `BaseModel` with `longest_first: tuple[tuple[str, ...], ...]`. The `PurePosixPath` field may need `arbitrary_types_allowed`, or the model can store `str` paths instead.

I checked the other listed criteria and found no further violations in the changed code:
- `from __future__ import annotations` is present.
- Type syntax is PEP 604 with no `typing.List` or `Optional`.
- Naming follows the snake_case, PascalCase and underscore-prefix rules.

<result>**Verdict: FAIL.** There is one blocking finding. I did not call `mark_review_as_passed`.

**Finding 1 (blocking), lines 487-489, `_CheckRun`**
- **Criterion violated:** "Value objects use `pydantic.BaseModel` with `model_config = ConfigDict(frozen=True)` and tuple (not list) sequence fields."
- **Offending code:** The frozen model `_CheckRun` was added by the HEAD commit (6a0ba8e). It declares its sequence fields as lists:
  ```python
  violations: list[Violation]
  reach_violations: list[ReachViolation]
  sdp_violations: list[SdpViolation]
  ```
  A `list` field on a frozen model can still be mutated in place, which defeats `frozen=True`. `python_conventions.md` names this as a non-negotiable convention.
- **Suggested fix:**
  - Declare the three fields as `tuple[Violation, ...]`, `tuple[ReachViolation, ...]` and `tuple[SdpViolation, ...]`.
  - In `_check_scan`, build them with `tuple(find_violations(...))`, `tuple(find_reach_violations(...))` and `tuple(find_sdp_violations(...))`. The `sdp_violations: list[SdpViolation] = []` local at line 521 becomes `()`.
  - Then check the consumers: `_violations_to_json`, `_violations_to_text`, `_reach_violations_to_json`, `_reach_violations_to_text`, `_sdp_violations_to_json`, `_sdp_violations_to_text` and `_transitive_unverified_reason`. `_transitive_unverified_reason` is annotated `list[Violation]`. Each one either accepts `Sequence[...]` or gets a `list(run.violations)` at the call site.

**What I checked and found clean**
- **Type syntax:** `from __future__ import annotations` is present, there is no `typing.List` or `Optional`, and there is no `Any`.
- **Line length:** no line exceeds 100 characters (checked with awk).
- **Paths:** `pathlib` is used throughout; there is no `os.path`.
- **Imports:** correctly grouped. The function-local imports are deliberate (lazy loading, or the call-time stub resolution that the comment at lines 2504-2507 explains).
- **`_HeaderRun`:** uses tuple fields and a frozen config.
- **Comments:** the changeset's comments explain why rather than what. I found no stale ones.

I also saw unannotated `s` parameters on `_refuse_vacuous_score`, `_echo_score` and `_record_score` (lines 684, 694, 707) and bare `dict` return types. These are not among the listed criteria, so I did not count them as findings.</result>

<result>**Review of `/Users/hosanglee/archy/AGENTS.md`: no Critical or High findings. Four Medium and Low issues, listed below.**

The file is well structured. Its hard rules come first, it gives a reason for each rule, and its commands are concrete. Because Medium and Low findings exist, I did not call `mark_review_as_passed`.

1. **"Tests that cannot fail" (line ~183 to 188) and "Two traps … A third"**
   - Severity: Low
   - Best practice: clarity, consistent structure.
   - Issue: The text says "Two traps" and then lists a third as "A third:". The count is wrong, and the list is buried in a dense paragraph, so an agent will skim it.
   - Fix: Say "Three traps" and format them as a bullet list like the "three shapes" list above them.

2. **Duplicated rules across sections**
   - Severity: Low
   - Best practice: avoid redundancy and drift risk.
   - Issue: Several rules are stated two or three times.
     - The `gh api` REST rule appears in Hard rules and again in "Git and GitHub".
     - "Never push to main" appears in both of those places too.
     - "Open the failing check, never the tally" appears in Hard rules and in the "Before you push" section.
     - The `ruff check` and header rules are each stated in both the hard rules and the explanatory paragraphs.
   - Fix: Keep the hard rules as the single source and have the later sections point back to them. Alternatively, drop the "Git and GitHub" section except for the stacked-PR note.

3. **Hard rules, lines 57 to 59**
   - Severity: Low
   - Best practice: avoid mixed signals.
   - Issue: The text says "All five are equally binding", then says "The first two lead because they go wrong quietly". This is slightly confusing about whether the order matters. The next paragraph also says rules elsewhere can be judged "rather than following it blindly". That could be misread as loosening the hard rules.
   - Fix: Say that order carries no priority, and add one sentence saying that only the rules outside this list are judgment calls.

4. **"Adding a gate or a payload field", line 114**
   - Severity: Medium
   - Best practice: clarity and structure.
   - Issue: One very long sentence packs about six file paths and parentheticals. The key action (grep for every site that renders the gate) is hard to extract. The "three surfaces" are never named in the text itself. They appear only in the image alt text, which a text-only agent may not use.

  ```python
  external = {n for n, d in g.nodes(data=True) if d.get("external")}
  g.remove_nodes_from(external)
  return g
  ```
  This reimplements `archy.graph_assemble.internal_subgraph`. That function's docstring says it exists "so the definition of 'external' lives in one place". The same file already imports and uses `internal_subgraph` in `_write_cyclic_ratchet_project`.
- **Suggested fix:** Make it `return internal_subgraph(build_graph(root))`. If the copy is deliberate, so the parity test derives the answer independently of the code under test, say that in the comment. The current comment says only "same internal-only graph the MCP layer hands to callers", which does not explain the choice.

**Checks that passed**
- `from __future__ import annotations` is present at module top.
- PEP 604 syntax is used, with no `typing.List` or `Optional`.
- Naming follows the conventions.
- There are no value-object classes, so the pydantic rule does not apply.

<result>**Verdict: FAIL.** There is one DRY finding. Every other criterion passes.

**Finding 1: lines 104-125, DRY**
- **Criterion violated:** "DRY: no copy-pasted blocks that should be a shared helper".
- **Offending construct:** `project_with_caller` (lines 115-125) repeats `acyclic_project` (lines 105-111) line for line. Both create `pkg/`, write an empty `__init__.py`, write `a.py` as `from pkg.b import thing\n`, and write an empty `b.py`. `project_with_caller` then adds `c.py`.
- **Suggested fix:** Build `project_with_caller` on top of `acyclic_project`, so the shared setup lives in one place:
  ```python
  @pytest.fixture
  def project_with_caller(acyclic_project: Path) -&gt; Path:
      # (keep the existing WHY comment)
      (acyclic_project / "pkg" / "c.py").write_text("from pkg.a import other\n")
      return acyclic_project
  ```
  This works because both fixtures resolve to the same `tmp_path` within a test.

**Criteria that pass:**
- `from __future__ import annotations` is present.
- Type syntax is PEP 604 style, with no `typing.List` or `Optional`.

- Imports are grouped as stdlib, third-party, then local.
- No line exceeds 100 characters. The longest are around 90.
- The comments and docstrings explain why (the #699 file-descriptor rationale and the shared-fixture intent).
